First, the wiggles obey a wave equation with a stiffness term (Klein–Gordon). The field's constitution already contains the particle; everything below works out those two sentences.</p>
<h2 id="what-a-field-is">What a field is</h2>
<p>In particle mechanics the dynamical variables are positions — finitely many numbers per instant. A <strong>field</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>x</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi(t,\vec x)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.714em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.2077em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11
10.667 11 18 0 6-1 10-3 12s-6.667 5-14 9c-28.667 14.667-53.667 35.667-75 63
-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> is one number <em>per point of space, per instant</em>. The clean mental model: a long vector <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_i(t)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">q</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em;"><span mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> indexed by lattice sites, then send the lattice spacing to zero, so the sum over sites <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation class="mord mathnormal">i</span></span></span></span> becomes an integral over <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>x</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\vec x</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.714em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.714em;"><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.2077em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5
3.333 2.667 6.667 9 10 19 6.667 24.667 20.333 43.667 41 57 7.333 4.667 11

-1.333 1.333-3.167 3.5-5.5 6.5s-4 4.833-5 5.5c-1 .667-2.5 1.333-4.5 2s-4.333 1
-7 1c-4.667 0-9.167-1.833-13.5-5.5S337 184 337 178c0-12.667 15.667-32.333 47-59
H213l-171-1c-8.667-6-13-12.333-13-19 0-4.667 4.333-11.333 13-20h359
c-16-25.333-24-45-24-59z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. A <strong>scalar</strong> field carries no spacetime indices — two Lorentz observers agree on its value at each fixed spacetime point. That makes it the cleanest place to learn the field-to-particle dictionary: no polarization labels to track yet, unlike the photon and graviton later in this module.</p>
<blockquote>

(Many texts use the opposite, mostly-minus signature — flip signs when cross-reading.)</p>

<h2 id="building-the-action">Building the action</h2>
<p>Mechanical intuition, imported term by term:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Kinetic energy</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>∼</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>rate of change</mtext><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim(\text{rate of change})^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.3669em;"></span><span class="mrel">∼</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0641em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">rate of change</span></span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>: it costs energy for the field to change in time. <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mtext>  </mtext><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\;T=\tfrac12(\partial_0\phi)^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1901em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" mtight">0</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span <li><strong>Potential energy</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>∼</mo><mo harmonic well pinning <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>. <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mtext>  </mtext><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" </ul>
<p>Both terms are quadratic in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span> and must share units, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>m</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>L</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[m]=L^{-1}=M</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span></span></span></span>: <strong>the constant <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span></span></span></span> carries units of mass</strong> — the first hint it will <em>be</em> the particle's mass.</p>
<p>The naive density <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" <strong>not</strong> Lorentz invariant: it singles out time. Relativity refuses to let time-variation cost energy while space-variation is free, so we must add a <strong>gradient energy</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tfrac12(\nabla\phi)^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> — spatial wiggles cost energy too, entered with the sign of ordinary potential energy. The time and space derivatives then merge into one contracted term:</p>

<p>Every index is contracted, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal">L</span></span></span></span> is a Lorentz scalar — that was the whole point of adding the gradient term. mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" <p>"<strong>Free</strong>" means every term is quadratic in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span>. In linear-algebra language <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span> is a quadratic form, so its equation of motion is linear and Fourier modes will diagonalize it, exactly as eigenvectors diagonalize a finite quadratic form. A cubic-or-higher term would couple the modes — an <em>interacting</em> field.</p>
<h2 id="equation-of-motion">Equation of motion</h2>
<p>Vary <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi\to\phi+\delta\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span>; only the derivative term needs care:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>∫</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>D</mi></msup><mi>x</mi><mtext> </mtext><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">(</mo><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext> </mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>ϕ</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta S
= \int d^Dx\,\Big(-\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu(\delta\phi)\,\partial_\nu\phi - m^2\phi\,\delta\phi\Big).</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8913em;"><span style="top:-3.113em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" <p>Integrate the derivative term by parts,</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext> </mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mtext> </mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><mo fence="true">)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu(\delta\phi)\,\partial_\nu\phi
=-\partial_\mu\!\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta\phi\,\partial_\nu\phi\right)
+\delta\phi\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\phi ,</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" <p>and the total derivative integrates to a boundary term that vanishes (compact support, falloff at infinity, or the periodic box used later). What survives is</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>∫</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>D</mi></msup><mi>x</mi><mtext>  </mtext><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mtext> </mtext><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">(</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>ϕ</mi><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta S = \int d^Dx\;\delta\phi\,\Big(\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\phi - m^2\phi\Big).</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1945em;position:relative;top:-0.0006em;">∫</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" mtight">μ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span <p>Requiring <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta S=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> for <em>arbitrary</em> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span> forces the bracket to vanish. Writing <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>≡</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>μ</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial^2\equiv\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≡</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span <p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><menclose notation="box"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.59em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></p>
<p>Separating time and space (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span mtight">t</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>) this is the <strong>Klein–Gordon equation</strong>:</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi <p>Read it as a wave equation with a stiffness term <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m^2\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0085em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span>: the stiffness makes different wavelengths travel at different speeds (dispersion), and that dispersion <em>is</em> the relativistic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span></span></span></span>–<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> relation we extract next.</p>
<blockquote>

A vacuum exists to build particles on top of.</p>

<p>Each derivative pulls down a factor of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation style="height:0.854em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>⋅</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>p</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial^2 e^{ip\cdot x}=(ip)\cdot(ip)\,e^{ip\cdot x}=-p^2\,e^{ip\cdot x}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8247em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8247em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0191em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8247em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">p</span><span class="mbin mtight">⋅</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">x</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, and Klein–Gordon becomes, mode by mode,</p>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace width="1em"></mspace><mtext>for every </mtext><mi>p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(p^2 + m^2)\,\phi(p) = 0 \quad\text{for every } p.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0641em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0641em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:1em;"></span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">for every </span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></p>
<p>This is now <strong>algebra, not calculus</strong>: a number times <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi(p)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> must vanish. So either <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi(p)=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>, or</p>
<p><span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \boxed{\ p^2 + m^2 = 0 \iff E^2 = \vec p^{\,2}+m^2 \iff E = \pm E_p\,,\quad E_p\equiv\sqrt{\vec p^{\,2}+m^2}\ } \tag{1}</span></p>
<p>This locus is the <strong>mass-shell</strong> (see <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/08-relativistic-point-particle/mass-shell-relation/" class="wiki-link">Mass-shell relation</a>): two sheets, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>p</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E=+E_p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9694em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">p</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>p</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E=-E_p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9694em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">p</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, exchanged by <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>→</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p\to-p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>. The classical field lives <em>only</em> on it: off-shell the amplitude is forced to zero, on-shell it is free, subject only to the reality condition <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>∗</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi(p)^*=\phi(-p)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> (automatic for a real <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi(x)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>). One complex amplitude therefore fixes the field at the two on-shell points <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> — the negative-energy sheet carries no independent data — so on average there is <strong>one real degree of freedom per point of the mass-shell</strong>, the phrase that generalizes to "<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D-2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> per point" for the photon and graviton in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-i-just-in-time-physics/04-classical-fields-modes-and-particles/polarizations-of-massless-spin-1-and-spin-2/" class="wiki-link">Polarizations of massless spin-1 and spin-2</a>.</p>
<p>Nothing here was quantized and no particles were assumed. A free scalar field is just a relativistic spring mattress, yet its own equation of motion refuses any wave whose <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>E</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>p</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(E,\vec p)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">E</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.714em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1522em;"><span class="overlay" style="height:0.714em;width:0.471em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.471em" height="0.714em" style="width:0.471em" viewBox="0 0 471 714" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin"><path d="M377 20c0-5.333 1.833-10 5.5-14S391 0 397 0c4.667 0 8.667 1.667 12 5

Quantization (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-i-just-in-time-physics/04-classical-fields-modes-and-particles/fourier-modes-as-oscillators/" class="wiki-link">Fourier modes as oscillators</a> and onward) will promote each allowed wave to a countable excitation — a particle. The field already knew the particle's energy.</p>
<h2 id="the-light-cone-form-preview">The light-cone form (preview)</h2>
<p>In light-cone coordinates <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>±</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>±</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^\pm=\frac{1}{\sqrt2}(x^0\pm x^1)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7713em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">±</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.3831em;vertical-align:-0.538em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.551em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord sqrt mtight"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9128em;"><span class="svg-align" style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mtight" style="padding-left:0.833em;">2</span></span><span style="top:-2.8728em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="hide-tail mtight" style="min-width:0.853em;height:1.08em;"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="400em" height="1.08em" viewBox="0 0 400000 1080" preserveAspectRatio="xMinYMin slice"><path d="M95,702

the second-order equation into a <strong>first-order, Schrödinger-like</strong> equation in light-cone time:</p>
<p><span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \Big(i\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^+} - \frac{1}{2p^+}\big(p^I p^I + m^2\big)\Big)\,\phi(x^+,p^+,\vec p_T) = 0. \tag{2}</span></p>

Developed in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-ii-classical-strings/12-classical-light-cone-gauge/light-cone-gauge/" class="wiki-link">Light-cone gauge</a>.</p>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>For <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#x3C;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m^2&#x3C;0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8532em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">&#x3C;</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> the algebraic mass-shell (1) still holds, but the potential <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>ϕ</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tfrac12 m^2\phi^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1901em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> turns into a hilltop: the Hamiltonian is no longer bounded below at <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>, so the field wants to roll away. This is the string tachyon; see <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iii-quantization/16-bosonic-spectrum-synthesis/tachyon-as-instability/" class="wiki-link">Tachyon as instability</a>.</li>
<li>Which boundary conditions legitimize dropping the boundary term? Compact support, falloff at infinity, or the periodic box of <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-i-just-in-time-physics/04-classical-fields-modes-and-particles/fourier-modes-as-oscillators/" class="wiki-link">Fourier modes as oscillators</a> — each closes the integration-by-parts.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>
<p>The mass-shell (1) was extracted from the covariant equation. Recover it instead from the <strong>light-cone</strong> equation (2), and read off what plays the role of energy.</p>
<p>Show that the plane wave <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>∝</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>p</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mi>T</mi></msub><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>x</mi><mo>⃗</mo></mover><mi>T</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\phi\propto e^{-ip^+x^- - ip^-x^+ + i\vec p_T\cdot\vec x_T}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∝</span><span Then explain in one sentence why <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> deserves the name "light-cone energy."</p>
<details class="callout callout-success">
<summary class="callout-title">Solution</summary>
<p>In light-cone coordinates the metric gives <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>I</mi></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>I</mi></msup></mrow><annotation style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">+</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="katex-mathml"><math <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>I</mi></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>I</mi></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace separator="true">,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-2p^+p^- + p^Ip^I + m^2 = 0 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad 2p^+p^- = p^Ip^I + m^2,</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">2</span><span
